\scriptstyle{(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{f}{T})\circ(\,_{f}{T}\ast\,_{Y}{T})}
\textstyle{\,{}_{X}{T}\circ F(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,{}_{X}{\ell}\ast F(f)}
\textstyle{\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)}

commutes, which holds because
(F(f),\,_{f}{T})
is a (strong) tangent morphism and

\,{}_{f}{T^{2}}=(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{f}{T})\circ(\,_{f}{T}\ast\,_{Y}{T}).

∎

Corollary 3.26
.

Let
F
be a tangent indexing functor. Then there is a vertical lift natural transformation
\underline{\ell}\colon\underline{T}\Rightarrow\underline{T^{2}}
.

Proof.

Apply Theorem
2.6
to the vertical lift modification
\ell\colon T\Rrightarrow T^{2}
.
∎

Lemma 3.27
.

Let
F
be a tangent indexing functor on
X
and let
A
be an object of
\mathbf{PC}(F)
. Then
(\underline{\ell}_{A},0_{A})
is a bundle morphism in
\Cscr
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A\times_{A}\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A\times_{\underline{T}A}\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{p}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{T}\ast\underline{p})_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{+}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\underline{\ell}_{A}\circ\pi_{1},\underline{\ell}_{A}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{T}\ast\underline{+})_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{0}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{0}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{T}\ast\underline{0})_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{0}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}_{A}}

all commute in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
. However, because these amount to checking the commutativity of diagrams in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
, it suffices to check that the diagrams all commute object-locally. That is, it suffices to prove that for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
, the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{\ell}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{p}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{X}{p})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{0}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)\times_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)\times_{\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{+}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\,_{X}{\ell}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\,_{X}{\pi_{1}},\,_{X}{\ell}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\,_{X}{\pi_{2}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{X}{+})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{\ell}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{0}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{0}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{X}{0})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{\ell}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}

all commute. However, this follows by Axiom 3 of Definition
3.1
applied to
(F(X),\,_{X}{\Tbb})
.
∎

Lemma 3.28
.

Let
F
be a tangent indexing functor on
X
. There is then a canonical flip modification
c\colon T\Rrightarrow T^{2}
as in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\underset{c}{\Rrightarrow}

whose object-local natural transformations are given by the canonical flips

\,{}_{X}{c}\colon\,_{X}{T^{2}}\to\,_{X}{T^{2}}

of the tangent structure
(F(X),\,_{X}{\Tbb})
.

Proof.

To show that
c
determines a modification it suffices to prove that for any
f\colon X\to Y
in
\Cscr_{1}
the diagram of functors and natural transformations

\textstyle{F(f)\circ\,_{Y}{T^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F(f)\ast\,_{Y}{c}}
\scriptstyle{\,{}_{f}{T^{2}}}
\textstyle{\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,{}_{X}{c}\ast F(f)}
\textstyle{F(f)\circ\,_{Y}{T^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,{}_{f}{T^{2}}}
\textstyle{\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)}

commutes. However, this is equivalent to asking that the diagram

\textstyle{F(f)\circ\,_{Y}{T^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F(f)\ast\,_{Y}{c}}
\scriptstyle{(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{f}{T})\circ(\,_{f}{T}\ast\,_{Y}{T})}
\textstyle{\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,{}_{X}{c}\ast F(f)}
\textstyle{F(f)\circ\,_{Y}{T^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{f}{T})\circ(\,_{f}{T}\ast\,_{Y}{T})}
\textstyle{\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)}

commutes, which holds because
(F(f),\,_{f}{T})
is a tangent morphism and because

\!\,{}_{f}{T^{2}}=(\!\,_{X}{T}\ast\!\,_{f}{T})\circ(\!\,_{f}{T}\ast\!\,_{Y}{T}).

∎

Corollary 3.29
.

For any tangent indexing functor
F
on
X
there is a canonical flip natural transformation
\underline{c}\colon\underline{T}^{2}\Rightarrow\underline{T}^{2}
.

Lemma 3.30
.

Let
F
be a tangent indexing functor on
X
and let
A
be an object of
\mathbf{PC}(F)
. Then
(\underline{c}_{A},\id_{\underline{T}A})
is a bundle morphism in
\Cscr
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A\times_{\underline{T}A}\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A\times_{\underline{T}A}\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}\underline{p}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{p}_{\underline{T}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{T}\ast\underline{+})_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\underline{c}_{A}\circ\pi_{1},\underline{c}_{A}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{+}\ast\underline{T})_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{T}\ast 0)_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{\underline{T}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0\ast\underline{T})_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{\underline{T}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}_{A}}

all commute in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
. However, because these amount to checking the commutativity of diagrams in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
, it suffices to check that the diagrams all commute object-locally. That is, it suffices to prove that for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
, the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{c}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}(\,_{X}{p}_{\,{}_{X}{A}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{p}\ast\,_{X}{T})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{\,{}_{X}{T}\,_{X}{A}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)\times_{\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)\times_{\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{T}\ast\,_{X}{+})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\,_{X}{c}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\,_{X}{\pi_{1}},\,_{X}{c}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\,_{X}{\pi_{2}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{+}\ast\,_{X}{T})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{c}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{T}\ast 0)_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{\,{}_{X}{T}\,_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0\ast\,_{X}{T})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{c}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}

all commute. However, this follows by Axiom 4 of Definition
3.1
applied to
(F(X),\,_{X}{\Tbb})
.
∎

Proposition 3.31
.

Let
F
be a tangent indexing functor. Then the canonical flip and vertical flip transformations satisfy the following coherences:
\underline{c}^{2}=\iota_{\underline{F}}
,
\underline{c}\circ\underline{\ell}=\underline{\ell}
, and the diagrams of functors and natural transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}\ast\underline{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}\ast\underline{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}\ast\underline{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}\ast\underline{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}\ast\underline{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}\ast\underline{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}\ast\underline{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}\ast\underline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}\ast\underline{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}\ast\underline{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}\ast\underline{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}\ast\underline{\ell}}

all commute.

Proof.

It suffices through Theorem
2.6
to show that the corresponding identities and commuting diagrams hold for the corresponding pseudonatural transformations and modifications. In particular, it suffices to verify each corresponding identity in
F(X)
for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
. However, each identity holds because each object is given in terms of the corresponding functor/natural transformation in the tangent structure
(F(X),\,_{X}{\Tbb})
.
∎

As a last lonely proposition we show the universality of the vertical lift in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
. This is our final ingredient in proving that
\mathbf{PC}(F)
carries the structure of a tangent category.

Proposition 3.32
.

Let
F
be a tangent indexing functor on
X
. Then for any
A\in\mathbf{PC}(F)_{0}
the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}_{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{T}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\underline{T}\ast\underline{+}\right)_{A}\circ\left\langle\underline{\ell}_{A}\circ\pi_{1},(\underline{0}\ast\underline{T})_{A}\circ\pi_{2}\right\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{T}\ast\underline{p})_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{0}_{A}\circ\underline{p}_{A}\circ\underline{p}_{\underline{T}A}}

is an equalizer in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
.

Proof.

Recall that because each pair
(F(f),\,_{f}{T})
for
f\in\Cscr_{1}
is a strong tangent morphism, all the pullbacks expressing the universality of the vertical lift are preserved by
F(f)
. Thus in order to prove the given diagram is an equalizer it suffices by Theorem
2.8
to check that for every
X\in\Cscr_{0}
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T_{2}}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}^{2}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\,{}_{X}{T}\ast\,_{X}{+}\right)_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\left\langle\,{}_{X}{\ell}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\pi_{1},(\,_{X}{0}\ast\,_{X}{T})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\pi_{2}\right\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,_{X}{T}\ast\underline{p})_{\,{}_{X}{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{0}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\underline{p}_{\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\underline{p}_{\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}}

is an equalizer. However, this holds because
(F(X),\,_{X}{\Tbb})=(F(X),\,_{X}{T},\,_{X}{p},\,_{X}{+},\,_{X}{0},\,_{X}{\ell},\,_{X}{c})
is a tangent category.
∎

With this we can finally prove that when
F
is a tangent indexing functor the pseudolimit category,
\mathbf{PC}(F)
is a tangent category.

Theorem 3.33
.

If
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
is a tangent indexing functor then
\mathbf{PC}(F)
is a tangent category where the tangent structure
\Tbb
is given by
\Tbb=(\underline{T},\underline{p},\underline{0},\underline{+},\underline{\ell},\underline{c})
for the functors and natural transformations determined in Lemma
3.11
and in Corollaries
3.14
,
3.16
,
3.23
,
3.26
,
3.29
.

Proof.

The existence of each functor and natural transformation is given in the statement of the theorem, so we only need to prove the desired coherences and structural properties of Definition
3.1
. However, prove the axioms in Definition
3.1
hold for
(\mathbf{PC}(F),\Tbb)
as follows:

(1)

Part
1
is verified by the existence of
\underline{T}
(cf. Lemma
3.11
), the existence of
\underline{p}
(cf. Corollary
3.14
), the existence of pullback powers of
\underline{p}_{A}\colon\underline{T}A\to A
for all
A\in\mathbf{PC}(F)_{0}
(cf. Corollary
3.20
), and the commutativity of composite powers of
\underline{T}
with pullback powers of
\underline{p}
(cf. Corollary
3.21
).

(2)

Part
2
holds by the existence of the bundle addition map
\underline{+}
(cf. Corollary
3.23
), the existence of the bundle unit
\underline{0}
(cf. Corollary
3.16
), and Proposition
3.24
.

(3)

Part
3
is verified in two steps. First, the existence of
\underline{\ell}
follows from Corollary
3.26
. Second, each pair
(\underline{\ell}_{A},\underline{0})_{A}
is a bundle map for all
A\in\mathbf{PC}(F)_{0}
by Lemma
3.27
.

(4)

Part
4
is verified in two steps. First, the canonical flip
\underline{c}
exists by Corollary
3.29
. Second, the pair
(\underline{c}_{A},\id_{\underline{T}A})
is a bundle map for any
A\in\mathbf{PC}(F)_{0}
by Lemma
3.30
.

(5)

Part
5
holds by Proposition
3.31
.

(6)

Part
6
holds by Proposition
3.32
.

Thus
(\mathbf{PC}(F),\Tbb)
is a tangent category.
∎

Example 3.34
.

Let
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
be any pseudofunctor and equip each fibre category
F(X)
with the trivial tangent structure
\,{}_{X}{I}
. Then the pseudolimit tangent structure
(\mathbf{PC}(F),{\Ibb})
asserted by Theorem
3.33
coincides with the trivial tangent structure
(\mathbf{PC}(F),\Ibb_{\mathbf{PC}(F)})
on
\mathbf{PC}(F)
.

For further (families of) examples of pseudolimit tangent structures, see Definitions
6.16
and
7.10
below.

3.3.
Tangent Objects in the Hom-2-Category

We close this section with a discussion of the theory of tangent objects and the relation of tangent indexing functors and tangent objects in the hom
2
-category
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
. Recently, in
[
16
]
Lanfranchi has defined the notion of a tangent object within a
2
-category as a natural generalization of what it means to be a tangent category; in fact, Lanfranchi’s definition is motivated by characterizing the tangent objects of
\fCat
as precisely tangent categories.
We begin by showing that the tangent objects of
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
can be described in terms of pseudofunctors
F:\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
which factor through the forgetful
2
-functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan_{\operatorname{strong}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}

and so we characterize the indexed tangent categories of
[
16
, Definition 3.8]
as exactly the tangent objects in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
4
4

4

In
[
16
]
, the definition of an indexed tangent category involves a pseudofunctor
F:\Cscr^{\op}\to\fTan_{\operatorname{strong}}
where
\Cscr=(\Cscr,\Tbb)
is some given tangent category. However, the definition involves no other use of the tangent structure on
\Cscr
and so may be omitted.
. To prove this, we begin with a convenient and important technical lemma.

Lemma 3.35
.

Let
(\Cscr,\Tbb)
and
(\Dscr,\Sbb)
be tangent categories. Then a morphism
(F,\alpha):\Cscr\to\Dscr
is a strong lax morphism of tangent categories if and only if
(F,\alpha^{-1}):\Cscr\to\Dscr
is a strong colax morphism of tangent categories.

Proof.

\implies
: If
(F,\alpha)
is a strong lax morphism of tangent categories then

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}

is a natural isomorphism. That this forms the natural transformation of a colax morphism of tangent categories follows by virtue of inverting the direction of all the maps
\alpha:F\circ T\Rightarrow S\circ F
together with the corresponding coherences.

\impliedby
: If we instead know that
(F,\alpha)
is a strong colax morphism of tangent categories, then

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}

is a natural isomorphism. That this forms the natrual transformation of a lax morphism of tangent categories follows by virtue of inverting the direction of all the maps
\alpha:S\circ F\Rightarrow F\circ T
together with the corresponding coherences.
∎

Because we require it in the remainder of this section and in Section
4
, we now recall the definition of what it means to be a tangent object in a
2
-category
\Cfrak
. This definition requires the use of
[
17
]
and the approach to tangent categories as categories
\Cscr
together with a
\mathbf{Weil}_{1}
-actegory structure, i.e., a strong monoidal functor
\mathbf{Weil}_{1}\to[\Cscr,\Cscr]
. We consequently also recall the definition of the category
\mathbf{Weil}_{1}
as well.

Definition 3.36

(
[
17
]
)
.

The category
\mathbf{Weil}_{1}
is the monoidal category where:

•

Objects: Finite products and tensor products of the
\N
-algebras

W^{n}:=\frac{\N[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}]}{(x_{i}x_{j}:1\leq i,j\leq n)}.

•

Morphisms: As in the category of augmented
\N
-algebras.

•

Composition: As in the category of augmented
\N
-algebras.

•

Identities: The identity of a Weil algebra
A
is
\id_{A}
.

•

Monoidal Product: The monoidal product is the
\N
-tensor product
\otimes_{\N}
.

•

Monoidal unit: The monoidal unit is
\N
.

•

Unitors: The tensor units
A\otimes_{\N}\N\cong A
and
A\cong\N\otimes_{\N}A
.

•

Associators: The tensor associator:
(A\otimes_{\N}B)\otimes_{\N}C\cong A\otimes_{\N}(B\otimes_{\N}C)
.

The category
\mathbf{Weil}_{1}
is equipped with a special algebra
W:=\N[x]/(x^{2})
which largely generates the category. It is used as a placeholder for determining the tangent structure on
\Cscr
by determining the functor where
W
is mapped to in the strong monoidal functor
\alpha:\mathbf{Weil}_{1}\to[\Cscr,\Cscr]
. The corresponding ways to translate the remainder of the tangent structure (namely the bundle projection, the sum, the zero, the lift, and the flip) are given by following the following morphisms:

•

p:W\to\N
induced by sending
x\mapsto 0
; this is the map which realizes
W
as an augmented
\N
-algebra.

•

The zero morphism
z:\N\to W
is the rig map which witnesses
W
as an
\N
-algebra.

•

The sum map
\operatorname{add}:W^{2}\to W
which sends the two generators
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
of
W^{2}
to the generator
x
of
W
.

•

The vertical lift
\ell:W\to W\otimes_{\N}W
is the map which sends the generator
x
of
W
to the element
x\otimes y
.

•

The canonical flip
c:W\otimes_{\N}W\to W\otimes_{\N}W
is defined to be the map

\frac{\N[x_{1},x_{2}]}{(x_{1}^{2},x_{2}^{2})}\to\frac{\N[x_{1},x_{2}]}{(x_{1}^{2},x_{2}^{2})}

given by
x_{1}\mapsto x_{2}
and
x_{2}\mapsto x_{1}
.

Generally we will denote by
p_{A}:A\to\N
the augmentation of the algebra
A
for all Weil algebras
A
.

Definition 3.37

(
[
16
]
, Definition 4.3)
.

Let
\Cfrak
be a
2
-category. We say that an object
X
of
\Cfrak
is a
tangent object
with a tangent structure
\Tbb
if there is a strong monoidal functor
F_{\Tbb}:\mathbf{Weil}_{1}\to\Cfrak(X,X)
for which:

(1)

F_{\Tbb}
preserves the pullbacks of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\times C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{A}\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{A}\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{A}\otimes p_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{A}\otimes p_{B}}

for all Weil-algebras
A,B,C
and that these pullbacks be pointwise, i.e., they are preserved by all functors
\Cfrak(f,X)
. Note that we also have omitted the unitor
A\otimes_{\N}\N\cong A
in the description of the pullback above.

(2)

The functor
F_{\Tbb}
perserves the universality of the vertical lift, i.e., if
\xi
is the pairing map of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\N\otimes_{\N}W^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes_{\N}W^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes_{\N}W^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes_{\N}W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ell_{W^{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z\otimes\id_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{W}\otimes\operatorname{add}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{W}\otimes\operatorname{add}}

then
F_{\Tbb}
preserves the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes_{\N}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1},x_{2}\mapsto 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{W}\otimes p_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

and sends it to a pointwise limit in
\Cfrak(X,X)
.

Remark 3.38
.

The definition of a tangent object is more general than we will require in this paper. What it means in the
2
-category is that to give a tangent object in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
is to give:

(1)

a pseudofunctor
F:\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat

a pseudonatural transformation and modification

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{F}}
\underset{p}{\Rrightarrow}

such that all iterated pullbacks against the cospan
T\xrightarrow{p}\id_{F}\xleftarrow{p}T
exists in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)(F,F)
and are preserved by all powers
T^{m}
. We define the pullback of
T\xrightarrow{p}\id_{F}\xleftarrow{p}T
to be the pseudonatural transformation
T_{2}
.

(2)

A pair of modifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\underset{\operatorname{add}}{\Rrightarrow}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\underset{0}{\Rrightarrow}

which make
(T,\operatorname{add}:T_{2}\to T,0:\id_{F}\to T)
into a commutative monoid in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)(T,T)
.

(3)

There are modifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\underset{\ell}{\Rrightarrow}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\underset{c}{\Rrightarrow}

which make the coherences in Lines (
3
) – (
7
) below hold.

(4)

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{add}\circ(\ell\times 0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\circ\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\ast p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

is a (pointwise) pullback in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)(F,F)
.

Below are the coherences we require the tangent objects to satisfy:

(3)

\displaystyle\ell\circ\operatorname{add}

\displaystyle=\left(\operatorname{add}\ast T\right)\circ\left(\ell\times\ell\right)

\displaystyle c\circ(T\ast\operatorname{add})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{add}\ast T)\circ(c\times c)

(4)

\displaystyle(p\ast T)

\displaystyle=T\ast p

\displaystyle 0\ast T

\displaystyle=c\circ(T\ast 0)

(5)

\displaystyle c\circ\ell

\displaystyle=\ell

\displaystyle c\circ c

\displaystyle=\id_{T^{2}}

(6)

\displaystyle(T\ast\ell)\circ\ell

\displaystyle=(\ell\ast T)\circ\ell

\displaystyle(\ell\ast T)\circ c

\displaystyle=(T\ast c)\circ(c\ast T)\circ\ell

(7)

\displaystyle(T\ast c)\circ(c\ast T)\circ(T\ast c)

\displaystyle=(c\ast T)\circ(T\ast c)\circ(c\ast T)

Remark 3.39
.

In the proof of the theorem below, we indicate where it is that we need to use pseudofunctors which land in
\fTan_{\operatorname{strong}}
and not merely lax functors which land in
\fTan
. The issue arises in each case where we invoke Lemma
3.35
, as this indicates that we require the naturality morphisms to be actual
isomorphisms
.

Theorem 3.40
.

Tangent objects in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
are determined by pseudofunctors
F:\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
which factor through the forgetful functor:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan_{\operatorname{strong}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}

Proof.

\implies:
Assume that
(F,T,p,0,\operatorname{add},\ell,c)
is a tangent object in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
. The object-local versions of the pasting diagrams and coherences required of a tangent object (cf. Lines (
3
) – (
7
)) imply that for every object
X\in\Cscr^{\op}
, the category
F(X)
is a tangent category with tangent functor
\,{}_{X}{T}
, bundle projection
\,{}_{X}{p}
, zero section
\,{}_{X}{0}
, addition
\,{}_{X}{\operatorname{add}}
, lift
\,{}_{X}{\ell}
, and canonical flip
\,{}_{X}{c}
. This shows us that the object-assignment
F_{0}:\Cscr^{\op}_{0}\to\fCat_{0}
factors as through
\Forget_{0}:\fTan_{0}\to\fCat_{0}
. To see that the morphisms
Ff:FY\to FX
are the functor components of (strong) lax tangent morphisms, recall that because
T:F\Rightarrow F
is a pseudonatural transformation for any
f:X\to Y
in
\Cscr
, there is a corresponding invertible
2
-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FX}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{Y}{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{f}{T}}

which, by virtue of the coherences implied in Lines (
3
) – (
7
), gives rise to a colax morphism of tangent categories. Moreover, because
T
is a pseudonatural transformation,
\,{}_{f}{T}
is invertible and so
(F(f),\,_{f}{T})
is a strong colax morphism of tangent categories. However, applying Lemma
3.35
shows that
(F(f),\,_{f}{T}^{-1})
is a strong lax morphism of tangent categories and hence shows that
F_{1}
factors through
\fTan
as well. Thus
F:\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
factors through
\Forget:\fTan_{\operatorname{strong}}\to\fCat
.

\impliedby
: Assume that
F:\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
factors as:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan_{\operatorname{strong}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}

Because
F
lands first in the
2
-category
\fTan_{\operatorname{strong}}
, for any morphism
f:X\to Y
in
\Cscr
there is a strong tangent morphism
(F(f),\,_{f}{\alpha})
. But then by Lemma
3.35
the map
\,{}_{f}{\alpha}^{-1}
fits into a
2
-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FX}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{Y}{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{f}{\alpha}^{-1}}

which varies pseudofunctorially in
\Cscr^{\op}
. Arguing as in Lemmas
3.11
shows that the data described by
T=(\,_{X}{T},\,_{f}{\alpha}^{-1})_{X\in\Cscr_{0},f\in\Cscr_{1}}
is then a pseudonatural transformation
T:F\Rightarrow F
. Similarly, arguing as in Lemmas
3.13
,
3.15
,
3.22
, Proposition
3.24
, Lemma
3.25
, and
3.28
show that the object-local assignments for the bundle projections
\,{}_{X}{p}
, the zero maps
\,{}_{X}{0}
, the additions
\,{}_{X}{\operatorname{add}}
, the lifts
\,{}_{X}{\ell}
, and the canonical flips
\,{}_{X}{c}
determine modifications
p
,
0
,
\operatorname{add}
,
\ell
, and
c
which determine a commutative monoid in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)(F,F)
, satisfy the coherences in Lines (
3
) – (
7
), and have universal vertical lifts. As such, by extracting this information we find that
F
determines a tangent object in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
.
∎

We now show how our notion of a tangent indexing functor fits into this formulation and language via the theorem below.

Theorem 3.41
.

Let
\Cscr
be a
1
-category and consider the
2
-category
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
. Then a tuple

(F,T,p,\operatorname{add},0,\ell,c)
in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
is a tangent object in the sense of
[
16
, Definition 3.2]
if and only if
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
is a tangent indexing functor.

Remark 3.42
.

Note that in Theorem
3.41
, the roles of the witness isomorphism of the pseudonatural transformation
\,{}_{f}{T}
and its inverse
\,{}_{f}{T}^{-1}
are reversed when passing between tangent indexing functors and tangent objects. This also illustrates another reason why we work with pseudonatural transformations as opposed to lax transformations.

Proof.

\impliedby:
Begin by observing that to define a tangent object, we need a
0
-cell
F
, a
1
-morphism
T\colon F\rightarrow F
, and
2
-morphisms
p\colon T\Rightarrow\id_{F}
,
0\colon\id_{F}\Rightarrow T
,
\operatorname{add}\colon T_{2}\Rightarrow T
,
\ell\colon T\Rightarrow T^{2}
,
c\colon T^{2}\Rightarrow T^{2}
which satisfy the appropriate generalizations of the tangent-category axioms described in Definition
3.1
. However,
0
-cells in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
are exactly pseudofunctors
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
,
1
-cells are exactly pseudonatural transformations between pseudofunctors, and
2
-cells are modifications so we are looking for a
7
-tuple of pseudofunctors, pseudonatural transformations, and modifications.

On one hand, if we have a tangent indexing functor
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
then we have a pseudonatural transformation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}

for which if
f\colon X\to Y
is a morphism in
\Cscr
,
(F(f),\,_{f}{T}^{-1})
is a strong tangent morphism. Lemmas
3.13
,
3.15
,
3.22
, and Proposition
3.24
show the existence of the modifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{F}}
\underset{p}{\Rrightarrow}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\underset{0}{\Rrightarrow}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\underset{\operatorname{add}}{\Rrightarrow}

The fact that for all
X
in
\Cscr_{0}
and for all
A
in
F(X)_{0}
, each tuple

\left(\,{}_{X}{p}_{A}\colon\,_{X}{T}(A)\to A,\,_{X}{\operatorname{add}_{A}}\colon\,_{X}{T_{2}}(A)\to\,_{X}{T}(A),\,_{X}{0_{A}}\colon A\to\,_{X}{T}(A)\right)

is a commutative monoid in
F(X)_{/A}
, it follows that
(T,p,\operatorname{add},0)
into a commutative monoid in the category
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)(F,F)
.

The existence of the vertical lift and canonical flip modifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\underset{\ell}{\Rrightarrow}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
\underset{c}{\Rrightarrow}

is precisely given in Lemmas
3.25
and
3.28
. That each of the required pasting equations, namely Lines (
3
) – (
7
) above,
all hold by virtue of the relevant pointwise versions of the equations invoked in Parts (1) through (5) of the proof of Theorem
3.33
. Finally, the fact that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{add}\circ(\ell\times 0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\circ\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\ast p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

is a pointwise pullback is equivalent to asking that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T_{2}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{(T\ast\operatorname{add})}\circ\langle\!\,_{X}{\ell}\circ\pi_{1},(0\ast T)\circ\pi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{(T\ast p)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{0}\circ\!\,_{X}{p}\circ\!\,_{X}{(p\ast T)}}

be an equalizer for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
and for all
A\in F(X)_{0}
. However, this diagram encodes exactly the universality of the lift
\ell
in each tangent category
F(X)
which gives the last remaining equation to show that
(F,T,p,\operatorname{add},0,\ell,c)
is a tangent object in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
.

\implies:
Assume that
(F,T,p,0,\operatorname{add},c,\ell)
is a tangent object in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
. Specifying the object-local version of the fact that
(T,p,\operatorname{add},0)
is a commutative monoid in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)(F,F)
and the various pasting diagrams described by the equations given in Lines (
3
), (
4
), (
5
), (
6
), and (
7
) above show that each category
F(X)
is a tangent category for each
X\in\Cscr_{0}
. As such, since we already have a pseudonatural transformation
T
at hand, we only need to show that for every
f\colon X\to Y
in
\Cscr
, there is a strong tangent morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T}\circ F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{f}{T}^{-1}}

This in turn amounts to proving that the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T}\circ F(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{T}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{p}\ast F(f)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\,_{Y}{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{Y}{T}\circ F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\ast\,_{Y}{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{0}\ast F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{f}{T}^{-1}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{T_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{X}{T_{2}}\circ F(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T}\circ F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{T_{2}}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{\operatorname{add}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\operatorname{add}}\ast F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{T}^{-1}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T}\circ F(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{T^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{T}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\ell}\circ F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{T}\ast\!\,_{f}{T}^{-1})\circ(\!\,_{f}{T}^{-1}\ast\!\,_{Y}{T})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{T^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{T^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{T^{2}}\circ F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{T}\ast\!\,_{f}{T}^{-1})\circ(\!\,_{f}{T}^{-1}\ast\!\,_{Y}{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{c}\ast F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{T}\ast\!\,_{f}{T}^{-1})\circ(\!\,_{f}{T}^{-1}\ast\!\,_{Y}{T})}

commute. However, the commutativity of each diagram is exactly equivalent to asking that
p\colon T\Rrightarrow\id_{F}
,
0\colon\id_{F}\Rrightarrow T
,
\operatorname{add}\colon T_{2}\Rrightarrow T
,
\ell\colon T\Rrightarrow T^{2}
, and
c\colon T^{2}\Rrightarrow T^{2}
are modifications. Thus
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
is a tangent indexing pseudofunctor with the roles of
\!\,{}_{f}{T}
and
\!\,{}_{f}{T}^{-1}
interchanged.
∎

4.
Pseudolimit Tangent Morphisms

So far we have seen in Theorem
3.33
that tangent indexing functors
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
give rise to tangent structures on their pseudolimit
\mathbf{PC}(F)
. In this section we will examine the functoriality of this construction based on the strict
2
-functor
\mathbf{PC}(-)\colon\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)\to\fCat
constructed in
[
25
, Lemma 4.1.11, Page 80]
; note that we have already seen the object assignment of
\mathbf{PC}(-)
in the construction of the pseudocone categories and
1
-and-
2
-cell assignments of
\mathbf{PC}(-)
in Theorem
2.6
. The content of the citation given here is that this construction is strictly
2
-functorial, i.e.,
\mathbf{PC}(-)
is a pseudofunctor with identity structure cells.

Our next main goal is to establish Theorem
4.9
, which states that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Tan}\left(\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}

exists, commutes strictly, and creates pseudolimits indexed by
1
-categories in the
2
-category
\fTan
. To do this, however, we will need to understand the
2
-category
\mathfrak{Tan}\left(\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)\right)
of tangent objects, tangent morphisms, and tangent transformations in the
2
-category
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
introduced in
[
16
]
.

We begin the task above by recalling what it means to be a morphism of tangent objects in the sense of
[
16
]
, as this will form the crux of our discussion of functoriality on
1
-cells and
2
-cells.

Definition 4.1

(
[
16
]
, Definition 4.15)
.

If
\Cfrak
is a
2
-category with tangent objects
(X,\Tbb)
and
(Y,\Sbb)
, then a
(lax) morphism of tangent objects

(X,\Tbb)\to(Y,\Sbb)
is a pair
(f,\alpha)
where:

(1)

f\colon X\to Y
is a
1
-cell in
\Cfrak
;

(2)

\alpha
is a
2
-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

(3)

The diagrams of
1
-cells and
2
-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\ast\!\,_{T}{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{S}{p}\ast f}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\ast\!\,_{T}{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{S}{0}\ast f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{2}\circ f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\ast\!\,_{T}{\operatorname{add}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{S}{\operatorname{add}}\ast f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{2}\circ f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\ast\!\,_{T}{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{S}{\ell}\ast f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S\ast\alpha)\circ(\alpha\ast T)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{2}\circ f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ T^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{2}\circ f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S\ast\alpha)\circ(\alpha\ast T)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\ast\!\,_{T}{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{S}{c}\ast f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S\ast\alpha)\circ(\alpha\ast T)}

each commute in the relevant hom-categories.

Specializing this to the
2
-category
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
we find the following characterization of (lax) tangent morphisms of tangent indexing functors. By Theorem
3.41
, this characterizes the tangent morphisms between tangent objects in the hom-
2
-category
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
F,G\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
be tangent indexing functors with tangent object structures
(F,T,\!\,_{T}{p},\,_{T}{0},\!\,_{T}{\operatorname{add}},\!\,_{T}{\ell},\,_{T}{c})
and
(G,S,\!\,_{S}{p},\!\,_{S}{0},\!\,_{S}{\operatorname{add}},\!|quot{\ell}{S},\!\,_{S}{c})
, respectively. To give a tangent morphism
(h,\alpha)\colon F\to G
it is necessary and sufficient to give a pseudonatural transformation
h\colon F\Rightarrow G
and a modificiation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h\circ T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ h}
\underset{\alpha}{\Rrightarrow}

such that for all objects
X
in
\Cscr
, the equations

(8)

\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\!\,_{X}{\left(\,{}_{T}{p}\right)}

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{\left(\!\,{}_{S}{p}\ast h\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\alpha}

(9)

\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(h\ast\!\,_{T}{0}\right)}

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{\left(\!\,{}_{S}{0}\ast h\right)}

(10)

\!\,{}_{X}{\left(\!\,{}_{S}{\operatorname{add}}\ast h\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{\alpha}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(h\ast\,_{T}{\operatorname{add}}\right)}

(11)

\!\,{}_{X}{\left(\!\,{}_{G}{\ell}\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{\left(S\ast\alpha\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(\alpha\ast T\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(\!\,{}_{F}{\ell}\right)}

(12)

\!\,{}_{X}{\left(S\ast\alpha\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(\alpha\ast T\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(\!\,{}_{T}{c}\right)}

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{\left(\!\,{}_{S}{c}\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(S\ast\alpha\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(\alpha\ast T\right)}

hold.

Proof.

To see that the equations are sufficient, we note that by requiring that each of the equations in lines (
8
) – (
12
) hold for each object
X\in\Cscr_{0}
, the coherence diagrams given in Item (3) of Definition
4.1
are all satisfied.

Alternatively, to see that giving a pseudonatural transformation
h\colon F\Rightarrow G
and modification
\alpha\colon h\circ T\Rrightarrow S\circ h
for which the equations given are necessary, we note that in order for Items (1) and (2) of Definition
3.3
to hold, we require a specified pseudonatural transformation
h
and modification
\alpha
of the tying declared here. Finally, each of the coherence diagrams in Item (3) of Definition
4.1
are described object-wise by the equations described in Lines (
8
) – (
12
), which shows their necessity.
∎

The proposition above allows us to deduce that giving tangent morphisms between tangent objects in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
is equivalent to giving a pseudonatural transformation and modification pair which is object-locally a tangent functor for every object in the base category
\Cscr
.

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
F,G\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
be tangent indexing functors with corresponding object-local tangent structures
(F(X),\!\,_{X}{\Tbb})
and
(G(X),\!\,_{X}{\Sbb})
, respectively. To give a tangent morphism
(h,\rho)\colon F\to G
is to give a pseudonatural transformation and modification pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cscr^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h\circ T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ h}
\underset{\alpha}{\Rrightarrow}

such that for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
the pair
(\!\,_{X}{h},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})
is a morphism of tangent categories.

Proof.

In both cases, we are given the existence of a psuedonatural transformation
h\colon F\Rightarrow G
and a modification
\alpha\colon h\circ T\Rrightarrow S\circ h
, so it suffices to verify that being a tangent morphism is equivalent to having each functor/natural transformation pair
(\!\,_{X}{h},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})
be a morphism of tangent categories. Observe, however, that the equations (
8
) – (
11
) precisely declare that each pair
(\!\,_{X}{h},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})
is a morphism of tangent categories, as they are exactly the coherences declared in Definition
3.3
. Thus declaring that the equations in Lines (
8
) – (
11
) hold for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
is equivalent to
(\!\,_{X}{h},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})
being a morphism of tangent categories for all objects.
∎

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
F,G\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
be tangent indexing functors and let
(h,\alpha)\colon F\Rightarrow G
be a tangent morphism between them in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
. Then the pair
(\underline{h},\underline{\alpha})\colon\mathbf{PC}(F)\to\mathbf{PC}(G)
is a morphism of tangent categories.

Proof.

Note that the content of this theorem amounts to proving that the diagrams of functors and natural transformations

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\circ\underline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}\circ\underline{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\ast\underline{p}_{\Tbb}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{p}_{\Sbb}\ast\underline{h}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\circ\underline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}\circ\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\ast\underline{0}_{\Tbb}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{0}_{\Sbb}\ast\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\alpha}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\circ\underline{T}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}_{2}\circ\underline{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\ast\underline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}\circ\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\ast\underline{\alpha}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\ast\underline{\operatorname{add}}_{\Tbb}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\operatorname{add}}_{\Sbb}\ast\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\alpha}}

(14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\circ\underline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}\circ\underline{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\circ\underline{T}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}^{2}\circ\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\ast\underline{\ell}_{\Tbb}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\ell}_{\Sbb}\ast\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{S}\ast\underline{h})\circ(\underline{\alpha}\circ\underline{T})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\circ\underline{T}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}^{2}\circ\underline{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\circ\underline{T}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{S}^{2}\circ\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{S}\ast\underline{h})\circ(\underline{\alpha}\circ\underline{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}\ast\underline{c}_{\Tbb}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{c}_{\Sbb}\ast\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\underline{S}\ast\underline{h})\circ(\underline{\alpha}\circ\underline{T})}

all must commute. However, to show this it suffices to prove that for any object
A=\{\!\,_{X}{A}\;|\;X\in\Cscr_{0}\}
of
\mathbf{PC}(F)
, the
X
-local versions of the diagrams above,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\circ\!\,_{X}{T}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\circ\!\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\!\,_{X}{{p}_{\Tbb}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{{p}_{\Sbb}}\ast\!\,_{X}{h}\right)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{h}\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\circ\!\,_{X}{T}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\circ\!\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\!\,_{X}{{0}_{\Tbb}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{{0}_{\Sbb}}\ast\!\,_{X}{h}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\right)_{2}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\right)_{2}\circ\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\!\,_{X}{T}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\circ\!\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}\right)_{2}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\!\,_{X}{\operatorname{add}_{\Tbb}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\operatorname{add}_{\Sbb}}\ast\!\,_{X}{h}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\circ\!\,_{X}{T}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\circ\!\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\right)^{2}\circ\!\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\!\,_{X}{{\ell}_{\Tbb}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{{\ell}_{\Sbb}}\ast\!\,_{X}{h}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{S}\ast\!\,_{X}{h})\circ(\!\,_{X}{\alpha}\circ\!\,_{X}{T})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\right)^{2}\circ\!\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(\!\,{}_{X}{S}\right)^{2}\circ\!\,_{X}{h}\right)\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{S}\ast\!\,_{X}{h})\circ(\!\,_{X}{\alpha}\circ\!\,_{X}{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{h}\ast\!\,_{X}{{c}_{\Tbb}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{{c}_{\Sbb}}\ast\!\,_{X}{h}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{S}\ast\!\,_{X}{h})\circ(\!\,_{X}{\alpha}\circ\!\,_{X}{T})}

all must commute; note that for readability, we have omitted the
\!\,{}_{X}{A}
subscript for the component of each natural transformation. However, each of these diagrams holds by virtue of the fact that
(h,\alpha)
is a tangent morphism, an application of Corollary
4.3
, and the fact that each
\!\,{}_{X}{A}\in F(X)_{0}
. As such, it follows that the diagrams expressed in Lines (
13
) and (
14
) commute and so that
(\underline{h},\underline{\alpha})
is a morphism of tangent categories.
∎

We now characterize the tangent transformations of
[
16
]
in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
and then prove that they become tangent natural transformations after applying the pseudocone
2
-functor
\mathbf{PC}(-)
. For this, however, we will start by recalling what it means to be a tangent transformation.

Definition 4.5

(
[
16
]
, Definition 4.16)
.

Let
\Cfrak
be a
2
-category with tangent objects
(X,\Tbb),(Y,\Sbb)
and with tangent morphisms
(h,\alpha),(k,\beta)\colon(X,\Tbb)\to(Y,\Sbb)
. A tangent transformation
\rho\colon(h,\alpha)\Rightarrow(k,\beta)
, described visually as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Tbb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,\Sbb)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(h,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(k,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

is given by a
2
-cell
\rho\colon h\Rightarrow k
for which the composite
2
-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h\circ T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{S}\ast\rho}

is equal to the composite
2
-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h\circ T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\circ T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\circ k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho\ast\id_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

Remark 4.6
.

The definition we have presented above is different from what is given in
[
16
]
, as it defines tangent transformations between two morphisms, from
(h,\alpha)
to
(k,\beta)
, and not between the composition of maps. The definition given in
[
16
]
gives the one presented above by setting either the vertical or horizontal morphisms to be identities.

Proposition 4.7
.

To give a tangent
2
-cell in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
of type

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(h,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(k,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

is equivalent to giving a modification
\rho\colon h\Rrightarrow k
such that for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
, the component natural transformation
\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}
is a tangent natural transformation.

Proof.

This is similar to proving Corollary
4.3
, and so some details are omitted. To this end, note that asking for the composites of modifications
(\id_{S}\ast\rho)\circ\alpha=\beta\circ(\rho\ast\id_{T})
is equivalent to asking that for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
, the equations

\left(\left(\id_{\!\,{}_{X}{S}}\right)\ast\!\,_{X}{\rho}\right)\circ\!\,_{X}{\alpha}=\!\,_{X}{\left(\id_{S}\ast\rho\right)}\circ\!\,_{X}{\alpha}=\!\,_{X}{\beta}\circ\!\,_{X}{\left(\rho\ast\id_{T}\right)}=\!\,_{X}{\beta}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\ast\left(\id_{\!\,{}_{X}{T}}\right)\right)

hold. However, each such equation is equivalent to
\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}
being a tangent natural transformation, which gives the proposition.
∎

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
F,G\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
be a tangent indexing functors with tangent morphisms
(h,\alpha),(k,\beta)\colon F\rightarrow G
and with a tangent
2
-cell
\rho\colon(h,\alpha)\Rightarrow(k,\beta)
. Then the induced natural transformation
\underline{\rho}
fitting into the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(G)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\rho}}

is a tangent natural transformation
\underline{\rho}\colon(\underline{h},\underline{\alpha})\Rightarrow(\underline{k},\underline{\beta})
.

Proof.

Note that to ensure that
\underline{\rho}
is a tangent transformation, we must show that the equation

(\id_{\underline{S}}\ast\underline{\rho})\circ\underline{\alpha}=\underline{\beta}\circ(\underline{\rho}\ast\id_{\underline{T}})

holds. To this end, let
A=\{\!\,_{X}{A}\;|\;X\in\Cscr_{0}\}
be an object in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
. By Proposition
4.7
we know that each
\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}
is a tangent natural transformation. As such, because morphisms in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
are determined by their object-local actions, it suffices to show the above identity holds for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
and for all component morphisms of the natural transformation. We now compute that the
\!\,{}_{X}{A}
-component of
(\id_{\underline{S}}\ast\underline{\rho})\circ\underline{\alpha}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left((\id_{\underline{S}}\ast\underline{\rho})\circ\underline{\alpha}\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}

\displaystyle=\left(\id_{\underline{S}}\ast\underline{\rho}\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\underline{\alpha}_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}=\left(\left(\id_{\!\,{}_{X}{S}}\right)\ast\!\,_{X}{\rho}\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}

\displaystyle=\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\beta}\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\ast\left(\id_{\!\,{}_{X}{T}}\right)\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}=\left(\underline{\beta}\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}\circ\left(\underline{\rho}\ast\id_{\underline{T}}\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}

\displaystyle=\left(\underline{\beta}\circ\left(\underline{\rho}\ast\id_{\underline{T}}\right)\right)_{\!\,{}_{X}{A}}

exactly because
\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}
is a tangent transformation. However, this implies that

\left((\id_{\underline{S}}\ast\underline{\rho})\circ\underline{\alpha}\right)_{A}=\left(\underline{\beta}\circ(\underline{\rho}\ast\id_{\underline{T}})\right)_{A}

and so proves that
\underline{\rho}
is a tangent transformation.
∎

We now collect our results above (namely Theorems
3.33
,
4.4
, and
4.8
) to give a strict
2
-functor

\mathbf{PC}\colon\mathfrak{Tan}\left(\mathsf{Bicat}\left(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat\right)\right)\to\fTan.

Theorem 4.9
.

The strict
2
-functor
\mathbf{PC}(-)
restricts to a strict
2
-functor
\mathbf{PC}\colon\mathfrak{Tan}\left(\mathsf{Bicat}\left(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat\right)\right)\to\fTan
and makes the diagram of
2
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Bicat}\left(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan\left(\mathsf{Bicat}\left(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}

commute strictly.

Proof.

The strictness of
\mathbf{PC}
is described in
[
25
, Lemma 4.1.11, Page 80]
. That it sends tangent objects in
\mathsf{Bicat}(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat)
to tangent categories is a combination of Theorems
3.33
and
3.41
, while the statement that
\mathbf{PC}
takes tanget morphisms to tangent functors is Theorem
4.4
and that
\mathbf{PC}
takes tangent
2
-cells to tangent natural transformations is Theorem
4.8
. As such, it follows that
\mathbf{PC}
restricts to

\mathbf{PC}\colon\mathfrak{Tan}\left(\mathsf{Bicat}\left(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat\right)\right)\to\fTan.

Finally, the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Bicat}\left(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan\left(\mathsf{Bicat}\left(\Cscr^{\op},\fCat\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fTan}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}

is immediate by construction.
∎

We now close this section with a discussion of pseudolimits indexed by
1
-categories in
\fTan
. In particular, we’ll show that if
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
is a tangent indexing functor then
\mathbf{PC}(F)
is the pseudolimit of the induced diagram
\tilde{F}\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fTan
. Afterwards, we’ll discuss how to use the strict
2
-functor
\mathbf{PC}(-)
to show that the forgetful functor
\Forget\colon\fTan\to\fCat
reflects and preserves (and hence creates) pseudolimits indexed by
1
-categories in
\fCat
.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
be a tangent indexing functor. The tangent category
\mathbf{PC}(F)
is the pseudolimit in
\fTan
of shape
F
.

Proof.

Our strategy here is to mimic the proof of the fact that
\mathbf{PC}(F)
is the pseudolimit in
\fCat
presented in
[
25
, Theorem 2.3.16, Page 43]
and adapt it to the tangent categorical situation. We do this in four main steps:

(1)

We show that for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
, the functor
\operatorname{pr}_{X}\colon\mathbf{PC}(F)\to F(X)
is a strict tangent functor.

(2)

We show that for all morphisms
f\colon X\to Y
in
\Cscr
, the
2
-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}}

is a tangent natural transformation
\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\colon(F(f)\circ\operatorname{pr}_{X},\!\,_{f}{T}\ast\operatorname{pr}_{X})\Rightarrow(\operatorname{pr}_{Y},\id)
.

(3)

We will show that if there is a tangent category
(\Dscr,\Sbb)
with tangent functors
\!\,{}_{X}{G}\colon\Dscr\to F(X)
for all objects
X\in\Cscr_{0}
and tangent natural transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{Y}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}}

which vary pseudofunctorially in
\Cscr^{\op}
, there is a tangent functor
G\colon\Dscr\to\mathbf{PC}(F)
for which the
2
-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{Y}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}}

pastes to the
2
-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{Y}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}}

(4)

Assume we have a tangent category
(\Dscr,\Sbb)
with two tangent morphisms
(G,\beta)\colon\Dscr\to\mathbf{PC}(F)
and
(H,\gamma)\colon\Dscr\to\mathbf{PC}(F)
and a family of tangent transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}}

for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
subject to the coherences that for all
f\colon X\to Y
in
\Cscr
,

\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\ast G\right)=\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\ast H\right)\circ\left(F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{\rho}\right).

We will then show that there exists a unique tangent transformation
P\colon(G,\beta)\Rightarrow(H,\gamma)
for which
\operatorname{pr}_{X}\ast P=\!\,_{X}{\rho}
.

We begin by proving (1). Fix an object
X\in\Cscr_{0}
and recall that the functor
\operatorname{pr}_{X}\colon\mathbf{PC}(F)\to F(X)
is defined by

\operatorname{pr}_{X}\left(\left\{\!\,{}_{Y}{A}\;|\;Y\in\Cscr_{0}\right\},\left\{\tau_{f}^{A}\;|\;f\in\Cscr_{1}\right\}\right)=\!\,_{X}{A}.

We then find that for all objects
A
of
\mathbf{PC}(F)
,

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{pr}_{X}\circ\underline{T}\right)(A)

\displaystyle=\left(\operatorname{pr}_{X}\left(\underline{T}\left\{\!\,{}_{Y}{A}\;|\;Y\in\Cscr_{0}\right\}\right)\right)=\operatorname{pr}_{X}\left\{\!\,{}_{X}{T}\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)\right\}=\!\,_{X}{T}\left(\!\,{}_{X}{A}\right)

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{T}\left(\operatorname{pr}_{X}\left\{\!\,{}_{Y}{A}\;|\;Y\in\Cscr_{0}\right\}\right)=\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{X}\right)(A),

proving that
\operatorname{pr}_{X}\colon(\mathbf{PC}(F),\Tbb)\to(F(X),\!\,_{X}{\Tbb})
is a strict tangent functor.

We now prove (2). Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in
\Cscr
and consider the
2
-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}}

Note that the natural transformation
\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}
is given via the assignment

\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\right)_{A}:=\left\{\tau_{f}^{A}\;|\;f\in\Cscr_{1}\right\}

for all objects
A
in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
; it is proved to be natural in
[
25
, Theorem 2.3.16, Page 81]
. With this in mind, we now must prove that
\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}
constitutes a tangent natural transformation
(F(f)\circ\operatorname{pr}_{X},\!\,_{f}{T}\ast\id)\to(\operatorname{pr}_{Y},\id)
. To this end, we compute that on one hand

\id_{\!\,{}_{X}{T}}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\ast\id_{\underline{T}}\right)_{A}=\id_{\!\,{}_{X}{T}}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\right)_{\underline{T}(A)}=\tau_{f}^{\underline{T}A}.

On the other hand,

\left(\id_{\!\,{}_{X}{T}}\ast\!\,_{f}{\alpha}\right)_{A}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{T}\ast\id_{\mathbf{PC}(F)}\right)_{A}=\!\,_{X}{T}\left(\tau_{f}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{T}\right)_{\!\,{}_{Y}{A}}=\tau_{f}^{\underline{T}A}

where the identity in the last equality sign is described in Remark
3.12
. As such, it follows that
\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}
is a tangent natural transformation.

We now prove (3). Assume that we have a tangent category
(\Dscr,\Sbb)
together with tangent functors
(\!\,_{X}{G},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})\colon\Dscr\to F(X)
for all objects
X\in\Cscr_{0}
and tangent natural transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{Y}{G},\!\,_{Y}{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{G},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F(f),\!\,_{f}{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}}

which vary pseudofunctorially in
\Cscr^{\op}
. Again guided by
[
25
, Theorem 2.3.16]
, we consider the comparison functor
G\colon\Dscr\to\mathbf{PC}(F)
defined by the object assignment

G(Z):=\left\{\!\,{}_{X}{G}\left(Z\right)\;|\;X\in\Cscr_{0}\right\}

with transition isomorphisms

T_{G(Z)}:=\left\{\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}_{Z}\;|\;Z\in\Cscr_{0}\right\}.

Let us now show that
G
is a part of a morphism of tangent categories. To do this define the natural transformation
\alpha\colon G\circ S\Rightarrow\underline{T}\circ G
by setting, for all
Z\in\Dscr_{0}
,

\alpha_{A}:=\left\{\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha_{Z}}\;|\;Z\in\Cscr_{0}\right\}.

If we can show that
\alpha
is a morphism in the category
\mathbf{PC}(F)
, the fact that
\alpha
is a natural transformation follows immediately from the naturality of the
\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}
. Similarly, if we know that
\alpha_{Z}
is a morphism in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
, then the fact that
\alpha
fits into a tangent morphism
(G,\alpha)
follows from the fact that each
(\!\,_{X}{G},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})
is a morphism of tangent categories. To this end, note that we must prove that for any
f\colon X\to Y
in
\Cscr
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\left(\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{G}\circ S\right)(Z)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\left(\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{T}\circ\!\,_{Y}{G}\right)(Z)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{G}\circ S\right)(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\circ\!\,_{X}{G}\right)(Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{f}^{G(SZ)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{\alpha}_{Z}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{f}^{\underline{T}(GZ)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}_{Z}}

commutes. Because on one hand we have that

\tau_{f}^{G(SZ)}=\!\,_{f}{\beta}^{SZ}=\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}\ast\id_{S}\right)_{Z}

and that

\tau_{f}^{\underline{T}(GZ)}=\!\,_{X}{T}\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}^{Z}\right)\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{T}\right)_{\!\,{}_{Y}{G}(Z)}.

So, using that
\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}
is a tangent natural transformation
\left(F(f)\circ\!\,_{Y}{G},\!\,_{f}{T}\ast\!\,_{Y}{\alpha}\right)\Rightarrow\left(\!\,{}_{X}{G},\!\,_{X}{\alpha}\right)
, we compute that

\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}_{Z}\circ\tau_{f}^{G(SZ)}

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{\alpha}_{Z}\circ\!\,_{f}{\beta}^{SZ}=\!\,_{X}{\alpha}_{Z}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}\ast\id_{S}\right)_{Z}=\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\alpha}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}\ast\id_{S}\right)\right)_{Z}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\ast\!\,_{f}{\beta}\right)\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{T}\ast\!\,_{Y}{\alpha}\right)\right)_{Z}=\left(\!\,{}_{X}{T}\ast\!\,_{f}{\beta}\right)_{Z}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{T}\ast\!\,_{Y}{\alpha}\right)_{Z}

\displaystyle=\!\,_{X}{T}\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}^{Z}\right)\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{T}\ast\!\,_{Y}{\alpha}\right)_{Z}=\!\,_{X}{T}\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}^{Z}\right)\circ\!\,_{f}{T}_{\!\,{}_{Y}{G}(Z)}\circ F(f)\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{\alpha}_{Z}\right)

\displaystyle=\tau_{f}^{\underline{T}(GZ)}\circ F(f)\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{\alpha}_{Z}\right).

This shows that
\alpha_{Z}
is a morphism in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
and hence proves that
(G,\alpha)\colon\Dscr\to\mathbf{PC}(F)
is a morphism of tangent categories. Finally, that
(G,\alpha)
makes the pasting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{Y}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}}

equal to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{Y}{G},\!\,_{Y}{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\!\,_{X}{G},\!\,_{X}{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F(f),\,_{f}{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{f}{\beta}}

is immediate from definition of all the morphisms in sight. As such, we have that
\mathbf{PC}(F)
is a pseudolimit in
\fTan
.

We now prove (4). Assume we have a tangent category
(\Dscr,\Sbb)
with two tangent morphisms
(G,\beta)\colon\Dscr\to\mathbf{PC}(F)
and
(H,\gamma)\colon\Dscr\to\mathbf{PC}(F)
and a family of tangent transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Dscr}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PC}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}}

for all
X\in\Cscr_{0}
subject to the coherences that for all
f\colon X\to Y
in
\Cscr
,

\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\ast G\right)=\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\ast H\right)\circ\left(F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{\rho}\right).

Let
A\in\Cscr_{0}
. To define
P\colon G\Rightarrow H
we set the
A
-component morphism
P_{A}\colon GA\to HA
to be given by

P_{A}:=\left\{\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)_{A}\colon\left(\operatorname{pr}_{X}\circ G\right)(A)\to\left(\operatorname{pr}_{X}\circ H\right)(A)\;:\;X\in\Cscr_{0}\right\}\colon GA\longrightarrow HA.

To see that this is a morphism in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
, recall that
\tau_{f}^{GA}=(\!\,_{f}{\alpha})_{GA}
and that
\tau_{f}^{HA}=(\!\,_{f}{\alpha})_{HA}
. We then find that the
A
-component of the required coherence

\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)_{A}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\ast G\right)_{A}=\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\ast H\right)_{A}\circ\left(F(f)\ast\!\,_{Y}{\rho}\right)_{A}

expands to the form

\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)_{A}\circ\tau_{f}^{GA}=\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)_{A}\circ\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\right)_{GA}=\left(\!\,{}_{f}{\alpha}\right)_{HA}\circ F(f)\left(\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{\rho}\right)_{A}\right)=\tau_{f}^{HA}\circ F(f)\left(\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{\rho}\right)_{A}\right)

which shows that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\left(\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{G}\right)A\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\left(\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{H}\right)A\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{G}\right)(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{H}\right)(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)\left(\left(\!\,{}_{Y}{\rho}\right)_{A}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{f}^{GA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{f}^{HA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)(A)}

commutes. Thus
P_{A}\colon GA\to HA
is a morphism in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
.

To see that the assignment
A\mapsto P_{A}
is natural in
\Dscr
, note that if
f\colon A\to B
is a morphism in
\Dscr
, the naturality of each component natural transformation
\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}
implies that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{G}\right)(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{H}\right)(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{G}\right)(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{H}\right)(B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{G}\right)(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{H}\right)(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)_{B}}

commutes. Because this holds for all
f\in\Dscr_{1}
, we have that
P
is a natural transformation with component morphisms
P_{A}
. That this is a tangent transformation follows from the fact that each
\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}
is a tangent transformation by an analogue of Proposition
4.2
and the fact that being a tangent transformation valued in
\mathbf{PC}(F)
can be checked object-locally. Moreover, a quick calculation for each object
X\in\Cscr_{0}
shows that for any object
A
of
\Dscr

\operatorname{pr}_{X}\ast P=\operatorname{pr}_{X}(P)=\!\,_{X}{\rho}.

That
P
is unique with this property is immediate to verify, as if there were any other tangent transformation
\Sigma\colon G\Rightarrow H
with
\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}=\operatorname{pr}_{X}\ast\Sigma
then we immediately have that for all objects
A
in
\Dscr
and for all objects
X
in
\Cscr
,

\left(\!\,{}_{X}{P}\right)_{A}=\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\rho}\right)_{A}=\left(\operatorname{pr}_{X}\ast P\right)_{A}=\left(\operatorname{pr}_{X}\ast\Sigma\right)_{A}=\left(\!\,{}_{X}{\Sigma}\right)_{A}.

Since each component of
\Sigma
agrees with each component of
P
, it follows that
P=\Sigma
and so
P
is unique.
∎

Corollary 4.11
.

The forgetful functor
\Forget\colon\fTan\to\fCat
reflects and preserves pseudolimits indexed by
1
-categories.

Proof.

An alternative way of phrasing this corollary is that for any tangent indexing functor
F\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fCat
with corresponding pseudofunctor
\tilde{F}\colon\Cscr^{\op}\to\fTan
induced by swapping the roles of
\!\,{}_{f}{T}
with
